# Day 1, 9:05, MAIL: from

Morgan Reyes - Manager

# Day 1, 9:05, MAIL: subject
[Story.DAYS.1.0.subject]
Your first day

# Day 1, 9:05, MAIL: body
[Story.DAYS.1.0.body]
Welcome to Pixel IT Partners. Alan and Garrison own the place and I run the floor for them. Tickets come in on ConnectWhy PSA, you fix things with LabRat RMM, and you only close a ticket once it is really fixed. Reopened tickets make me sad. Clients also call and text, so check your phone (P) now and then. - Morgan

# Day 2, 9:05, MAIL: from
[Story.DAYS.2.0.from]
Morgan Reyes - Manager

# Day 2, 9:05, MAIL: subject
[Story.DAYS.2.0.subject]
Daily chore: verify backups

# Day 2, 9:05, MAIL: body
[Story.DAYS.2.0.body]
Every morning open LabRat RMM, click BACKUPS and check each client. Backup jobs can fail silently and nobody notices until the day it really matters. It takes thirty seconds, until your security skills are good enough for the backup watch to do it for you. - Morgan

# Day 3, 9:05, MAIL: from
[Story.DAYS.3.0.from]
Morgan Reyes - Manager

# Day 3, 9:05, MAIL: subject
[Story.DAYS.3.0.subject]
Get good at PowerShell

# Day 3, 9:05, MAIL: body
[Story.DAYS.3.0.body]
The PowerShell Academy is on your desktop. Every lesson makes your remote fixes faster and more reliable, and later levels unlock scripts that fix a whole client at once. Study between tickets. - Morgan

# Day 4, 9:05, MAIL: from
[Story.DAYS.4.0.from]
Morgan Reyes - Manager

# Day 4, 9:05, MAIL: subject
[Story.DAYS.4.0.subject]
Windows XP is going away

# Day 4, 9:05, MAIL: body
[Story.DAYS.4.0.body]
Microsoft ends support for Windows XP and Office 2003 on April 8. After that there are no more security patches, so every XP machine at every client is a liability. Dr. Patel at Acme Dental is first. Survey the old PC (old software, old drivers, odd settings), back it up before you move anything, migrate, then verify with the user before you wipe it. - Morgan

# Day 5, 9:11, MAIL: from

# Day 5, 9:11, MAIL: body
[Story.DAYS.5.2.body]
You are on the pager next week (Monday to Friday nights). If a client alert wakes you, you can get up and fix it, check it first, or let it ride, but clients do not forgive a dead server. On-call pay is $40 a day. Leave for work early enough, too: Route 9 is down to one lane. - Morgan

# job: REMOTE ACCESS BREAK-IN, step 1

# job: REMOTE ACCESS BREAK-IN, step 2
[Playbook.BOOKS.rdp.steps.1.choices.0.note]
Port 3389 (remote desktop) is forwarded straight to the server. The whole internet can try to log in.

# job: REMOTE ACCESS BREAK-IN, step 2

# job: REMOTE ACCESS BREAK-IN, step 2

# job: REMOTE ACCESS BREAK-IN, step 3
[Playbook.BOOKS.rdp.steps.2.title]
CONTAIN

# job: REMOTE ACCESS BREAK-IN, step 3
[Playbook.BOOKS.rdp.steps.2.text]
Some of those thousands of guesses might have worked. Most attackers back then just guess common passwords.

# job: REMOTE ACCESS BREAK-IN, step 3
[Playbook.BOOKS.rdp.steps.2.choices.0.label]

# job: REMOTE ACCESS BREAK-IN, step 3
[Playbook.BOOKS.rdp.steps.2.choices.0.note]
Two accounts used passwords like Summer2013. One had been used from overseas last week: it relayed spam, nothing was copied.

# job: REMOTE ACCESS BREAK-IN, step 3

# job: REMOTE ACCESS BREAK-IN, step 4

[Playbook.BOOKS.rdp.steps.3.text]

# job: REMOTE ACCESS BREAK-IN, step 4
[Playbook.BOOKS.rdp.steps.3.choices.0.note]
Remote access now needs the VPN first. The guessing attacks hit a closed door.

# job: REMOTE ACCESS BREAK-IN, step 4
[Playbook.BOOKS.rdp.steps.3.choices.1.label]
FIREWALL THAT AUTO-BLOCKS REPEATED FAILED LOGINS

# job: REMOTE ACCESS BREAK-IN, step 4
[Playbook.BOOKS.rdp.steps.3.choices.1.note]
After a few bad guesses an address is blocked automatically. The attacks stop within the hour.

# job: REMOTE ACCESS BREAK-IN, step 4

# job: REMOTE ACCESS BREAK-IN, step 4

# job: WIRE REQUEST FROM THE BOSS

# job: WIRE REQUEST FROM THE BOSS, step 1
[Playbook.BOOKS.ceofraud.steps.0.title]
THE EMAIL

# job: WIRE REQUEST FROM THE BOSS, step 1
[Playbook.BOOKS.ceofraud.steps.0.text]
A paralegal at Hoot and Holler forwards an email that looks like it is from Atty. Spence: wire $38,000 from the client trust account today, quietly, no calls. She thinks it is odd. What do you do first?

# job: WIRE REQUEST FROM THE BOSS, step 1
[Playbook.BOOKS.ceofraud.steps.0.choices.0.label]
LOOK AT THE FULL EMAIL HEADERS

# job: WIRE REQUEST FROM THE BOSS, step 1
[Playbook.BOOKS.ceofraud.steps.0.choices.0.note]
The sender name says Spence. The reply address is spence@hootholler-law.example: a lookalike domain.

# job: WIRE REQUEST FROM THE BOSS, step 1
[Playbook.BOOKS.ceofraud.steps.0.choices.1.label]
CALL ATTY. SPENCE ON HIS CELL

# job: WIRE REQUEST FROM THE BOSS, step 1
[Playbook.BOOKS.ceofraud.steps.0.choices.1.note]
He never sent anything. He is in a deposition and furious that anyone would think so.

# job: WIRE REQUEST FROM THE BOSS, step 1
[Playbook.BOOKS.ceofraud.steps.0.choices.2.label]
REPLY AND ASK IF IT IS REAL

# job: WIRE REQUEST FROM THE BOSS, step 1
[Playbook.BOOKS.ceofraud.steps.0.choices.2.note]
'Yes, it is me. Please hurry.' The scammer answers in four minutes.

# job: WIRE REQUEST FROM THE BOSS, step 1
[Playbook.BOOKS.ceofraud.steps.0.choices.3.label]

# job: WIRE REQUEST FROM THE BOSS, step 1
[Playbook.BOOKS.ceofraud.steps.0.choices.3.note]

# job: WIRE REQUEST FROM THE BOSS, step 2
[Playbook.BOOKS.ceofraud.steps.1.title]
HOW DID THEY KNOW?

# job: WIRE REQUEST FROM THE BOSS, step 2
[Playbook.BOOKS.ceofraud.steps.1.text]
The email used the right wording, the right account and the right amount. Somebody has been reading the firm's mail. Where do you look?

# job: WIRE REQUEST FROM THE BOSS, step 2
[Playbook.BOOKS.ceofraud.steps.1.choices.0.label]
CHECK THE MAILBOX RULES AND RECENT LOGINS

# job: WIRE REQUEST FROM THE BOSS, step 2
[Playbook.BOOKS.ceofraud.steps.1.choices.0.note]
A hidden rule forwards every message mentioning wires to an outside address. The login that created it came from overseas.

[Playbook.BOOKS.ceofraud.steps.1.choices.1.label]

# job: WIRE REQUEST FROM THE BOSS, step 2
[Playbook.BOOKS.ceofraud.steps.1.choices.2.note]

[Playbook.BOOKS.ceofraud.steps.2.choices.2.note]

# job: WIRE REQUEST FROM THE BOSS, step 4
[Playbook.BOOKS.ceofraud.steps.3.text]

ALAN IS OUT
